## Operating directive for this mini log

1. Google Drive is **READ ONLY**. Do not create, edit, rename, move, upload, or delete any Drive file unless the user explicitly changes this instruction.
2. Use the active Sports Research Markdown framework in Google Drive as the governing research reference.
3. Append each new sports forecast/request to **this local Markdown running log** after completing the required research and before final delivery.
4. Continue canonical event numbering from the existing P-215/P-216 continuation. **Next new distinct event: P-217.**
5. Do **not** perform an automatic post-game retrospective or detailed settlement after each new forecast. The user will request retrospective/settlement work separately.
6. Do not rewrite an issued prediction. Corrections, state changes, settlements, and retrospectives are append-only when explicitly requested.
7. For each new event, preserve exact identity, game state, target, supplied contracts, source limitations, scenario tree/corridor, ranked picks, potential winner, and method/probability/value status.
8. Research must remain honest: no invented lineups, injuries, starters, prices, statistics, weather, rules, results, or probabilities.
9. When a decision-driving fact is unavailable or conflicting, record the missingness/conflict and reduce evidence quality rather than filling the gap.
10. The ranking remains a **marginal win-likelihood / robustness ranking**, not a staking, profitability, or expected-value claim unless the full value gate is separately satisfied.

## Participant / availability / role state

- **Guyana Amazon Warriors:** current squad sources include Shai Hope, Glenn Phillips, Shimron Hetmyer, Quentin Sampson, Mohammad Nabi, Romario Shepherd, Dwaine Pretorius, Shamar Joseph, Khary Pierre and Imran Tahir among the available group. These names are **squad/current-role context, not a confirmed XI** for this match.
- **Trinbago Knight Riders:** recent 2026 QPO XIs have featured Colin Munro, Alex Hales/Matthew Breetzke, Sunil Narine, Nicholas Pooran, Kieron Pollard, Matthew Tromp, Jyd Goolie, Dominic Drakes, Akeal Hosein, Usman Tariq and Abdul-Raheem Toppin. Today's XI remained unconfirmed at V01 cutoff.
- Because the exact XI/toss had not passed the official release handshake, participant-sensitive confidence is capped. No unverified starter/batting-order claim is treated as fact.

## Baseline and adjusted evidence

### Reference/base-rate / line anchor

- The most useful same-event market geometry found was Ladbrokes' **GAW team total 160.5**, with Over and Under both listed at 5/6 at capture. This is used only as an external same-event threshold anchor, **not** as an internal probability or proof of value.
- Ladbrokes also exposed GAW 0–6 alternate thresholds including **45.5, 50.5 and 55.5**. The snippet did not expose same-time prices for those specific phase alternates, so 45.5 is not treated as a precisely de-vigged fair line.
- User's full-innings line **174.5** is therefore 14 runs above the clearly balanced 160.5 same-event team-total benchmark.
- User's 6-over line **46.5** lies close to the range of the current GAW powerplay evidence and is not structurally generous in either direction.

### GAW full-innings recency windows — raw threshold audit

The following are **raw CPL team-innings outcomes**, not a calibrated base rate. Chase innings are target-capped and DLS/shortened innings are excluded where they break comparability.

| Window | Raw GAW innings >=175 | Raw GAW innings <=174 | Interpretation |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| L5 | 0 | 5 | Strong descriptive Under-174.5 frequency, but three current-2026 observations are successful chases with low targets |
| L10 | 2 | 8 | Under remains the more common raw outcome |
| L15 | 3 | 12 | Same direction; mixture spans current and prior CPL seasons |
| L20 eligible | 5 | 15 | 175+ remains a material tail, not the historical centre |

Key recent source-backed sequence used in those windows includes 2026 CPL innings of **118/4, 157/3 and 121/3** in successful chases, followed by 2025 CPL scores including **130/8, 157, 189/6, 188/8, 99, 144/8, 168/7, 170/6, 163/9 and 202/6**. The high scores prove that a 175+ branch is very real; the distribution is not a low-ceiling one.

### GAW powerplay current-regime evidence

The three completed GAW CPL 2026 powerplays that were directly recoverable before cutoff were:

- vs Jamaica Kingsmen: **45/3 after 6**
- vs Saint Lucia Kings: **48/0 after 6**
- vs Antigua & Barbuda Falcons: **55/2 after 6**

Raw L3 mean = about **49.3**, median = **48**, with two of three above the supplied 46.5 threshold. This is a very small sample and all three were chases, so it supports only a **slight opening-phase Over lean**, not strong confidence.

Exact phase-consistent L5/L10/L15/L20 powerplay windows were not fully reconstructable from a field-owned source before the cutoff. They are recorded as **missing rather than fabricated**, which lowers the evidence grade for C03/C04.

### Head-to-head / continuity

Current H2H source gives the last ten TKR–GAW meetings as **5–5**. GAW team scores in those ten were:
**130, 168, 163, 137, 148, 99, 166, 177, 175, 173**.

- At the 174.5 threshold: **2/10 were 175+** (177 and 175), **8/10 were 174 or lower**.
- Most recent three 2025 meetings: **130, 168, 163** — all below 174.5.
- QPO meetings in the current H2H window include **148/7 in 2024** and **175/4 in 2023**.
- Continuity is only moderate: several core names persist, but squads/roles and the 2026 venue/season regime are not identical. Older H2H therefore remains secondary evidence.

## Current team / opponent process

### Guyana Amazon Warriors batting

**Positive / upper-tail mechanisms**
- GAW entered this fixture after a 3–0 CPL start and have shown an explosive top/middle-order ceiling.
- In the Antigua chase, Hope accelerated late in the powerplay and GAW reached 55/2; Hetmyer then made 49 off 19 in a rapid 99-run stand.
- The 2025 CPL history contains 189/6, 188/8 and 202/6 innings, so a 175+ result is not an exotic outlier.
- A powerplay in the high 40s/low 50s leaves 175 attainable without requiring an absurd death-overs finish.

**Negative / Under mechanisms**
- The 2026 CPL sample entering this game has been **three chases**, all against modest targets. It does not establish a new 175+ batting-first central regime.
- Recent H2H against TKR has generally held GAW below this line.
- A middle-overs spin/control phase is a credible brake if Narine/Hosein and the recent QPO bowling structure are retained, but today's XI was not confirmed and therefore this mechanism is not upgraded to fact.

### Trinbago Knight Riders bowling / venue regime

Recent QPO results show large variance:
- TKR restricted Barbados to **127/9**, with Sunil Narine taking 2/14.
- TKR also conceded **211/4** to Saint Lucia Kings at the same venue.
- Against Jamaica on Aug 29, TKR's 180/6 was followed by 127/3 in 14.4 overs before a DLS result.

Therefore QPO/TKR cannot be modelled as automatically low-scoring. The opponent process supports both a strong suppression branch and a genuine 180–200+ concession tail.

## Environment / strip gate

- **Venue classification:** OUTDOOR.
- **STRIP STATUS:** `NOT FOUND AFTER SEARCH`. No exact-match field-owning strip report was available at the V01 cutoff. No grass/hardness/seam/turn claim is invented.
- **MATCH CONDITIONS STATUS:** `OBSERVED AT REGIONAL/GOVERNMENT LEVEL, EXACT GROUND VECTOR INCOMPLETE`.
- Trinidad & Tobago Meteorological Service showed about **27°C**, dew point **24°C**, easterly wind around **13 km/h**, with a partly cloudy/cloudy forecast, showers and a medium thunderstorm chance in the relevant forecast block.
- Exact near-bounce QPO wind vector relative to pitch orientation was not verified. Wind therefore widens uncertainty rather than being assigned a directional scoring effect.
- Rain/DLS risk is **bidirectional**, not an automatic Under: a shortened innings may change the offered/settled target or void treatment depending on operator rules, which were not supplied.

## Joint qualitative corridor / scenario map

No numerical probabilities are generated. The following is a qualitative shared innings tree.

### Lower-output branch — approximately 135–150
- Early wicket cluster or restrained powerplay around 38–44.
- TKR's recent QPO spin/control state persists through overs 7–15.
- GAW must rebuild rather than launch continuously.
- **Helps:** C02 Under 174.5, C04 Under 46.5.
- **Hurts:** C01 Over 174.5, C03 Over 46.5.

### Central branch — approximately 155–171
- Powerplay approximately 45–52.
- One useful top-order partnership but no sustained 10+ RPO middle/death surge.
- GAW retain wickets but TKR win enough middle-over phases.
- **Helps most:** C02 Under 174.5.
- C03/C04 depend on the exact first-six start.

### Upper-output branch — approximately 180–195+
- GAW reach roughly 50–60 in the powerplay with wickets in hand.
- Hope/Phillips/Hetmyer-type aggression survives into the middle overs and/or TKR miss lengths at the death.
- TKR's SLK-style high-concession branch reappears.
- **Helps:** C01 Over 174.5 and usually C03 Over 46.5.

### Fast-start / full-innings Under branch
This is particularly important because the two recommended directions are not contradictory:
- GAW can make **47–52 in six overs** and still finish **160–170** if TKR's spin/middle-overs control works.
- At 47 after six, GAW still need **128 from the final 14 overs** to reach 175, about 9.14 runs/over.
- At 50 after six, they need **125 from 14**, about 8.93 runs/over.
This is achievable, but it requires the innings to sustain or re-accelerate rather than simply having a good powerplay.

## Ranking logic

### #1 — GAW Under 174.5
This line is the most robust of the four. It sits materially above the same-event 160.5 balanced team-total reference, and recent GAW–TKR history plus the raw current/previous CPL innings windows put 175+ in the upper branch rather than the centre. The pick is **not** SUPPORTED/high-confidence because GAW have genuine 180–200+ batting capability, TKR conceded 211/4 at QPO recently, the exact strip was unavailable, and the final XI/toss were not field-owner confirmed.

### #2 — GAW Over 46.5 after six
Current GAW CPL powerplays of 45, 48 and 55 place 46.5 close to the opening-phase centre, with two of three clearing the line. Their top order has shown willingness to attack the new ball. This is only a **slight** lean: three games is too small a sample, all were chases, and today's opening pair/XI and exact strip were not confirmed.

### #3 — GAW Under 46.5 after six
The Under has a credible path through an early wicket cluster and TKR's QPO suppression state, but the available current-regime PP evidence marginally favours the opposite side. Because 46.5 is near the centre, ranks #2 and #3 are substantially closer to one another than either is to the full-innings extremes.

### #4 — GAW Over 174.5
This remains a legitimate upside scenario, not a dismissed possibility. GAW's lineup has the power to clear it and TKR's current defence has allowed a 211/4 innings at QPO. It ranks fourth because 175 requires the upper branch to persist across more than just a fast powerplay, while the most concrete same-event team-total anchor is 160.5 and the recent H2H/innings distribution is mostly below 175.

## Potential winner

**Guyana Amazon Warriors — full-match winner: LEAN**

Rationale:
- GAW entered unbeaten in their first three CPL 2026 matches and their bowling unit has recently combined early wickets, middle-overs spin control and high-end pace.
- The same-event Ladbrokes match-winner market had GAW at **4/5** versus TKR at **1/1**, an external market signal that also slightly favoured Guyana.
- TKR's home-QPO path is substantial: they recently restricted Barbados to 127/9, and their batting contains major match-winning power.
- H2H is balanced at **5–5 across the last ten**, so history does not justify a strong winner call.
- **Winner status therefore remains LEAN, not a lock and not a published probability.**

## Player / ranking / workload state

- **Ann Li:** current structured ranking feed places her around **WTA #30**; she is seeded in the US Open draw. She arrived after a deep Monterrey hard-court run, reaching the semifinal and losing a competitive 7-6, 6-4 match to Diane Parry.
- **Antonia Ruzic:** current ranking feeds place her in roughly the **low-80s**. Individual pages differed between #81 and #85 around the Monday ranking update; this minor ranking-version discrepancy is logged and not used as a decisive input.
- **Rest/workload:** Li played multiple Monterrey matches through Aug 28, creating slightly more recent workload but also much stronger match rhythm. Ruzic exited Monterrey in the first round on Aug 24 and enters with more rest but weaker recent match form.
- No verified current injury/withdrawal report was found for either player before issue.

- Correct-score market strongly prefers a Li straight-set win but does not make a 2-0 result equivalent to a -4.5 cover.

This market geometry agrees with the qualitative model: **Li is a clear match favourite, Li -4.5 is only modestly favoured, and 20.5 sits almost exactly at the match-length centre.**

## Serve / return matchup

### Ann Li
- Li's most recent Monterrey semifinal against Parry remained competitive despite defeat; she reached the tiebreak in set one and lost by only three total games.
- Immediately before that, she beat Parks 6-2, 6-3 and Osorio 6-1, 6-2.
- Against opponents below the elite Gauff/Rybakina tier, Li has recently shown the ability to convert return pressure into multi-break sets.

### Antonia Ruzic
- Ruzic's current hard-court serve has been the most important vulnerability. The Bartunkova, Frech and Keys matches all produced below-ordinary service-point survival and multiple-break exposure.
- Her ceiling remains real: earlier in 2026 she beat Qinwen Zheng 6-4, 7-5 at Indian Wells and came through Jennifer Brady in three sets. That prevents an aggressive assumption that Ruzic cannot take a set or keep both sets close.
- The model therefore retains both a **Li separation branch** and a **Ruzic-resistance / three-set branch**.

## Shared match score tree

### Branch A — Li controlled straight sets (highest-weight qualitative family)
Representative scores:
- **6-3, 6-4** → 19 games, Li +5
- **6-2, 6-4** → 18 games, Li +6
- **6-2, 6-3** → 17 games, Li +7

Helps:

- **Under 20.5**

Mechanism:
- Li wins second-serve exchanges, creates repeated break-point pressure and prevents Ruzic from stabilizing service games.

### Branch B — Li wins straight sets but Ruzic keeps them tight
Representative scores:
- **6-4, 6-4** → 20 games, Li +4
- **7-5, 6-4** → 22 games, Li +4
- **7-6, 6-4** → 23 games, Li +3

Helps:
- Ruzic +4.5 in every example
- Under wins only the 6-4, 6-4 branch
- Over wins 7-5/6-4 and 7-6/6-4

This branch is why a likely Li straight-set win does **not** mechanically validate Li -4.5.

### Branch C — three-set match
Typical total: normally well above 20.5.

Paths:
- Li loses a set but regains separation (e.g. 4-6, 6-2, 6-2) can still cover -4.5.
- A more balanced 6-4, 4-6, 6-4 style match strongly favours Ruzic +4.5 and Over 20.5.

Helps:
- **Over 20.5**
- Usually Ruzic +4.5, though not always.

### Branch D — Ruzic upset
- Ruzic's earlier 2026 Zheng/Brady results show this branch cannot be deleted.
- A close Ruzic upset strongly helps +4.5 and usually Over.
- A quick Ruzic straight-set upset can instead help Under.

## Ranking reasoning

### #1 — Ann Li -4.5 games

This is the best of the four because the current matchup mechanism can plausibly create **multiple-break separation**, and recent Ruzic hard-court losses have repeatedly landed beyond this exact boundary:
- -5 games vs Bartunkova
- -6 vs Frech
- -9 vs Keys

Li, meanwhile, has just produced +9 and +7 margins over Osorio and Parks and +5 over Zhang. The current external market also places Li -4.5 slightly on the favoured side of the handicap pair.

**Why not stronger than LEAN:** Li can win 2-0 and still fail this line. A 6-4, 6-4 result gives her the match but only +4 games. Ruzic has enough prior hard-court quality to make that close-set branch ordinary rather than remote.

### #2 — Under 20.5 games

The Under is aligned with the same primary match state but is not identical to the handicap:
- 6-4, 6-4 gives **Under 20.5** while Ruzic +4.5 wins.
- 6-3, 6-4 gives **Under 20.5 + Li -4.5**.

Ruzic's last three North-American hard losses were totals of **19, 20 and 15**, while Li's two most convincing Monterrey wins were 15 and 17 games. This supports a short-match branch.

**Kill path:** a third set makes the Over very difficult to avoid. Even a straight-set match with a tiebreak or two 7-5/6-4 type sets can cross 20.5. Current books priced the total almost exactly 50/50, so this remains only a slight Under lean.

### #3 — Over 20.5 games

The Over's best path is Ruzic resistance rather than a Ruzic outright win:
- one set for Ruzic almost automatically creates enough exposure;
- 7-5, 6-4 or 7-6, 6-4 also clear the total in straight sets.

Ruzic has beaten Zheng and Brady on hard in 2026 and has enough competitive ceiling to force this state.

It ranks below the Under because her **current** serve/return form has deteriorated across the last three North-American hard matches, and Li enters after two dominant Monterrey wins plus a competitive semifinal.

### #4 — Ruzic +4.5 games

This is a credible but fourth-ranked branch. The cushion wins on common Li scores such as 6-4, 6-4 and 7-5, 6-4, and it becomes strong if Ruzic takes a set.

However, the current-regime evidence directly attacks the contract: her last three hard-court losses were all beyond +4.5, while Li's recent win margins against non-elite opponents have often cleared five games. The lone H2H also finished 6-2, 6-2 to Li, although that old meeting is intentionally downweighted.

## Potential winner

**Ann Li — match winner (full completed match endpoint): LEAN / strongest side**

Main reasons:
1. Current rankings/seed position place Li materially above Ruzic.
2. Li enters with superior immediate hard-court form, including a Monterrey semifinal and dominant wins over Osorio/Parks.
3. Ruzic has lost three straight North-American hard matches in straight sets.
4. Ruzic's recent service-point survival has been poor against Bartunkova, Frech and Keys.
5. The external same-event market strongly favours Li, consistent with but not determinative of the internal qualitative conclusion.

Upset path:
- Ruzic protects second serve materially better than in her last three matches, extends early return games, and forces Li into the close/tiebreak or three-set branch. Her 2026 wins over Zheng and Brady prove that this state is not purely hypothetical.

## Joint run corridor / scenario tree

### A. Close Rays win — 4-3 or 5-4
Mechanisms:
- Seymour limits major damage without dominating.
- Stock/bullpen concede enough for Tampa to edge ahead, but no blowup.
- **Wins:** Mets +1.5.
- 4-3 helps Under; 5-4 helps Over.

### B. Low-total Rays separation — 4-1, 4-2, 5-2
Mechanisms:
- Seymour plus Rays bullpen suppress New York.
- Stock has an ordinary short outing rather than a catastrophic one.
- Tampa converts enough baserunners to separate.
- **Wins:** Rays -1.5 + Under 7.5 in 4-1/4-2/5-2.

This is the mandatory low-total separation path; low total does not imply Mets +1.5 safety.

### C. Stock hook/blowup + Rays separation — 6-2, 7-2, 7-3
Mechanisms:
- Stock's walk/hit cluster or early contact tail reappears.
- Tampa reaches the middle relief chain early.
- **Wins:** Rays -1.5 + Over 7.5 (except exact 6-2 is 8 and still Over 7.5).

### D. Competitive high-total — 5-4, 6-4, 6-5
Mechanisms:
- Seymour's recent HR susceptibility shows up.
- Mets score enough without Soto to pressure Tampa.
- **Usually wins:** Over 7.5.

## Ranking logic

### #1 — Mets +1.5
The Rays are the stronger winner side, but +1.5 is a materially easier contract than asking Tampa to win by two. Tampa's current four-game winning streak includes **two consecutive one-run wins**, and current same-event pricing also makes Mets +1.5 the highest-likelihood supplied threshold. The largest adverse mechanism is Stock's severe blowup tail, which is why this is only a LEAN.

### #2 — Over 7.5
The user's 7.5 threshold is favorable relative to a main market around 8.0. Stock has allowed 8 ER and 7 ER in two of five starts and consistently creates early bullpen exposure; Seymour has also allowed meaningful recent HR damage. The Over can win through both a Tampa blowout and a competitive 5-4 state. Its main kill path is a Rays-controlled 4-1/4-2 game.

### #3 — Rays -1.5
This has very clear matchup support: Rays top-order quality, Stock's short-start/blowup distribution, Soto absent from the Mets lineup, and strong historical Rays separation in the most recent interleague series. It ranks below Mets +1.5 because a one-run Rays win is common and sufficient to kill -1.5, and below Over because the 7.5 total line has more winning score families.

### #4 — Under 7.5
The Under is absolutely plausible: Seymour/Rays bullpen can suppress New York, and Tampa does not need to explode to win. But its ordinary kill paths are broader than the other three: Stock can produce a multi-run inning, Seymour has recently leaked homers, and even 5-3 / 6-2 already clear 7.5. It therefore ranks fourth, not because low scoring is impossible but because the supplied threshold is demanding.

## Potential winner

**Tampa Bay Rays — full-game moneyline winner: LEAN**

Primary reasons:
1. Strong starter-quality/command advantage: Seymour over Stock.
2. Tampa owns the stronger current offense and enters 82-54.
3. Rays top three remain intact; Soto is not in the Mets starting lineup.
4. Stock's short-start profile increases exposure to the New York bullpen.
5. Tampa's home record and current four-game winning streak support the baseline.

Upset path:
- Stock lands in his 4–5 inning one-run branch, Seymour's recent HR susceptibility appears, and the Rays' high-leverage bullpen is less effective after the prior 11-inning game.

## Ranking logic

### #1 — Reds +1.5
The Padres are the better winner side, but Cincinnati +1.5 captures every Reds victory and every one-run Padres win. San Diego has played many close games: in the last ten, the opponent-side +1.5 analogue would have covered 8/10. Singer also held San Diego to 2 earned runs in six innings in June. The main failure path is King controlling Cincinnati while the Reds' weak recent relief pitching creates late 2+ separation.

### #2 — Over 9.0
The total is an integer line, so exactly nine pushes. The immediate Reds regime is highly volatile but currently power-heavy: 10, 10, 5 and 7 runs in four of the last five games, including four home runs yesterday. Singer's August ERA-level output is ordinary rather than dominant, Cincinnati's relief form is poor, and the weather is hot. The Under remains very live because King is the strongest run-prevention component and San Diego's relievers have been excellent.

### #3 — Padres -1.5
This is the best pure matchup-mechanism side: King > Singer, Padres bullpen > Reds bullpen, and San Diego retains its top offensive core. A 5-2/6-2 state is very plausible. It ranks below Reds +1.5 because the line requires two-run separation, while Singer's June performance against San Diego and Cincinnati's current offense create many one-run/competitive branches.

### #4 — Under 9.0
King can absolutely anchor a 4-2 or 5-2 game, and Padres recent broad total windows have leaned low. But at this exact venue/date, Cincinnati's current HR surge, Singer's moderate run allowance, weak Reds relief form, hot conditions and San Diego's recently taxed late bullpen widen the 10+ tail enough that the Over gets the slight preference.

## Potential winner

**San Diego Padres — full-game winner: LEAN**

Primary reasons:
1. Clear starting-pitcher advantage with Michael King.
2. Stronger current bullpen quality, despite workload concerns.
3. Reds relief pitching is the most vulnerable unit in the game.
4. San Diego still fields Tatis, Machado, Merrill, Bogaerts and France.
5. Current market baseline also makes San Diego the modest favourite.

Upset path:
- Singer repeats his June success against San Diego, King gives Cincinnati 3–4 runs, De La Cruz/Stewart/Stephenson/Suárez exploit the hot hitting environment, and the Padres' fatigued late relief chain fails to protect a narrow lead.

## Ranking logic

### #1 — Nationals +1.5
This is the most forgiving contract. Miami is the preferred outright winner, but Washington +1.5 captures every Nationals win and every one-run Marlins win. The current series has already produced a 5-4 Washington extra-inning win and yesterday's game was 2-0 Washington entering the ninth before the bullpen collapse. The main failure path is obvious and serious: Dion's workload forces an early transition into the league's weaker current bullpen, and Gusto suppresses Washington enough for Miami to separate late.

### #2 — Over 8.5
The Over is supported by the unusual pitching architecture more than by raw recent totals. Dion is not a normal six-inning starter; a 65–75 pitch cap can expose four-plus innings of a Washington bullpen carrying a 5.43 ERA over the last 15 days. Nationals Park's 2026 Statcast factor is strongly hitter-friendly, especially for home runs, and the forecast is warm/humid. Gusto's recent results are excellent but his underlying contact estimates are notably less dominant, preserving a Washington scoring branch.

### #3 — Under 8.5
The Under is close to #2 and has strong mechanisms: Gusto's current form, the Marlins' better bullpen, the absence of Xavier Edwards from Miami's order, and the fact that 6/10 recent H2Hs stayed below nine runs. It ranks below the Over because Washington's bullpen exposure is unusually large, Nationals Park is playing hitter-friendly in 2026, and exact lineup uncertainty prevents confidently suppressing Washington's scoring ceiling.

### #4 — Marlins -1.5
Miami absolutely has a 5-2/6-2 path and has covered -1.5 in 7 of the last 10 meetings. Gusto also recently threw four scoreless innings against Washington. But -1.5 is a strict separation contract: any Washington win and every one-run Miami win lose. The same-event market places this branch materially below Nationals +1.5, and the current series has produced enough Washington resistance that it remains the hardest of the four supplied contracts.

## Ranking logic

### #1 — Mariners +1.5
Boston is the preferred outright winner, but Seattle +1.5 captures every Mariners win and every one-run Boston win. The current external base-rate anchor strongly favours +1.5 over Boston -1.5, and Kirby's latest start plus his solid career history against Boston preserve a genuine close-game state.

The main reason this is not stronger is Seattle's bullpen. If Tolle keeps Seattle near 1–2 runs and Kirby exits with a close game, Boston has a major late separation edge against a relief corps running above a 7 ERA in the current window.

### #2 — Over 7.5
The user's 7.5 line is half a run below the main market total of 8.0. Seattle's immediate offense is cold, but the Over does not require Seattle to score heavily: Boston can carry a 6-2/7-2 result through Kirby's volatility and the Seattle bullpen. Boston's own lineup is weakened by Contreras' IL placement and Rutschman sitting, which prevents a stronger Over grade.

### #3 — Under 7.5
The Under has an excellent first-six-inning mechanism: Tolle is in strong current form, Kirby just delivered a dominant outing, Seattle has four runs across its last four games, and the latest June meeting with Tolle ended 3-1. It ranks only slightly below the Over because the Seattle relief chain is unusually fragile and 7.5 leaves no push protection at exactly eight.

### #4 — Red Sox -1.5
The pure matchup mechanism is strong: Boston has the better current starter form, better bullpen, home field and stronger team baseline. Boston won two of three in Seattle in June, both wins by four runs. But -1.5 requires separation and loses on every one-run Boston win. Kirby's quality-start branch and Boston's depleted lineup keep this as the hardest supplied condition even though Boston is the preferred winner.

## Ranking logic

### #1 — Phillies +1.5
Philadelphia is already a slight moneyline favourite in the same-event market. Giving that side an additional 1.5 runs creates the broadest supplied winning set: every Phillies win plus every one-run loss. Current team form (8-2 L10 / four straight), Nola's major recent ERA improvement and the bullpen edge all support the contract.

### #2 — Diamondbacks +1.5
This remains a strong contract because Arizona is essentially near-even on the moneyline, has a 40-29 home record and starts Pfaadt. All three 2026 H2Hs were one-run games, so both +1.5 sides would have cashed in all three. It ranks below Philadelphia +1.5 because the Phillies are the stronger current team state and own the post-starter bullpen advantage.

### #3 — Under 8.5
The Under is supported by both starters' current form, the controlled roof environment and the current-season H2H shape. It is not stronger because Nola's season-level ERA remains poor, Pfaadt has allowed nine earned runs in his last two starts and Arizona's bullpen has a 4.88 ERA over the last 15 days.

### #4 — Over 8.5
The Over is fully plausible. Both offenses average about 4.53–4.54 runs/game season-wide, and Arizona relief creates a late run-cluster route. It ranks fourth because tonight's current starting-pitcher state is more suppressive than the season scoring baseline, and the closed roof removes environmental volatility.

**Philadelphia Phillies — LEAN**

Primary reasons:
1. Wolfram current record: 77-60 vs Arizona 73-65.
2. Phillies are **8-2 L10** and on a four-game winning streak.
3. Nola's current form is dramatically better than his season ERA.
4. Philadelphia's recent bullpen is stronger than Arizona's.
5. Same-event market makes Philadelphia the slight road favourite.

Arizona upset path:
- Pfaadt returns to his early-August shutout state;
- Arizona's home offense, potentially strengthened by Marte's reinstatement, reaches Nola for one early cluster;
- Arizona avoids exposing the weaker portions of its bullpen.

Winner call is therefore **Philadelphia LEAN**, not a high-confidence lock.

Tseng has substantially deeper Challenger/ATP experience, a career high of #83, and just reached a Challenger final. Zhang's recent 4-1 form is genuine, but most of it came at M15 level. The raw 5/5 +4.5 coverage is therefore shrunk rather than carried forward mechanically.

- Zhang's recent indoor-hard matches show enough first-serve stability to force tiebreak/close-set states, but second-serve and return quality are more vulnerable when opponent level rises.

## Ranking logic

### #1 — Under 20.5
The strongest shared state is still Tseng winning in straight sets. Under 20.5 survives both a dominant Tseng cover (6-3,6-3) and a closer Tseng non-cover (6-4,6-4), making it more robust than choosing one handicap side. The main failure route is Zhang extending a set to 7-5/7-6 or taking a set.

### #2 — Tseng -4.5
The level gap and Zhang fatigue are decisive counterweights to Tseng's weak hard-court record. A 6-3,6-3 / 6-3,6-4 / 6-2,6-4 state is plausible and aligned with the same-event market. Confidence is capped because Tseng covered -4.5 in only 1/6 verified 2026 outdoor-hard matches.

### #3 — Zhang +4.5
The strongest evidence against Tseng -4.5 is meaningful: Zhang would have covered +4.5 in all five recent matches, Tseng is only 2-4 on 2026 outdoor hard, and common Tseng wins such as 6-4,6-4 or 7-5,6-4 cash the underdog handicap. It stays below Tseng -4.5 because that form came at M15 indoor-hard level after five consecutive match days.

### #4 — Over 20.5
The Over becomes strong if Zhang takes a set or forces long sets, and 3/5 of Zhang's latest matches cleared 20.5. It ranks fourth because the level-adjusted central branch is still Tseng 2-0, with many ordinary straight-set scores finishing at 17–20 games.

## Potential winner

**Chun-Hsin Tseng — LEAN / strongest side**

Reasons:
1. No. 3 seed and a roughly 500+ ranking-position established-level advantage.
2. Career high #83 and much deeper Challenger/ATP experience.
3. Four wins en route to the Prague Challenger final nine days ago.
4. Zhang is moving directly from five consecutive M15 match days into a higher-level event.
5. Same-event market strongly favours Tseng.

Upset path: Tseng's weak 2026 outdoor-hard form persists, Zhang transfers his indoor-hard serving stability cleanly outdoors, and Tseng fails to create the expected break density.

## Important limitations

1. Scheduled-start feeds conflict and the nominal start window had already begun/passed; current sources still showed **UPCOMING / 0-0** at freeze, so no live score was invented.
2. Wolfram had no direct current entity record for these players/event; it was used for arithmetic only.
3. Exact ATP ranking numbers vary across current weekly snapshots; only the robust ranking/experience gap is used.
4. Zhang's recent five-match sample is **indoor hard M15**, not outdoor-hard Challenger.
5. Tseng's latest excellent form is **clay**, not hard.
6. No H2H exists.
7. Operator retirement/void rules are unknown.
8. Weather forecasts differ on shower timing.
9. No fitted/validated tennis probability model exists.

## Bottom line

1. **Under 20.5 Games**
2. **Tseng -4.5 Games**
3. **Zhang +4.5 Games**
4. **Over 20.5 Games**
5. **Potential winner: Chun-Hsin Tseng**

## Ranking logic

### #1 — Hiroshima +1.5
The contract covers every Hiroshima win plus every one-run Chunichi win. Hiroshima has won 13 of 19 season meetings, while the same-event market prices the +1.5 cushion much more strongly than the standard side. The main kill path is Ono dominance plus Tokoda regression producing 3-1/4-1 Chunichi.

### #2 — Under 6.5
Both clubs are 7/10 Under in their most recent ten game windows, Ono is in a strong current state, and Tokoda has a 2.74 direct-matchup ERA despite his recent overall regression. The strict 6.5 threshold is the risk: 4-3 loses immediately, and the renovated dome no longer deserves an extreme pitcher-park assumption.

### #3 — Chunichi +0.5
This aligns with the preferred winner and is protected by an NPB draw under ordinary half-run grading, subject to operator rules. Ono, home field, team pitching and current market all favour Chunichi modestly. It ranks below Hiroshima +1.5 because any Hiroshima win loses +0.5, whereas Hiroshima +1.5 survives both Hiroshima wins and one-run Chunichi wins.

### #4 — Over 6.5
The Over has real evidence: season H2H averages 7.16 runs and recent meetings include 10-1, 8-1, 7-1 and 11-4. Tokoda also enters off a 5-ER start. It remains fourth because the current starter matchup and immediate team-total windows concentrate more heavily in 2-1, 3-1 and 3-2 families.

## Potential winner

**Chunichi Dragons — LEAN**

Reasons:
1. Ono is the stronger current starter.
2. Chunichi owns the slightly better season run differential / pitching baseline.
3. Home field at Vantelin Dome.
4. Current same-event moneyline makes Chunichi the modest favourite.

Counter-case: Hiroshima is **13-6 head-to-head in 2026**, Tokoda has pitched well against Chunichi specifically, and the two clubs are nearly tied in the standings. This is therefore only a LEAN.

### #4 — Under 6.5
Under has a coherent path through Ito repeating his Aug. 25 command/contact outcome and Kuri reverting toward his stronger season baseline. But 6.5 is a tight number: any 4-3 result loses, and recent Orix/Rakuten games plus H2H have been decisively more Over-heavy than Under-heavy.

## Ranking logic

### #1 — Hanwha +1.5
This is ranked first because +1.5 absorbs every Hanwha win and every one-run KT win. White is substantially better than Hanwha's staff-wide ERA and gives the Eagles a real five-to-six-inning competitive base. The same-event line is also priced far shorter than any other supplied market.

The warning is severe: Hanwha +1.5 is only 0/5 and 5/20 in recent windows, and KT has covered -1.5 in 7/11 season H2Hs. The pick is therefore a **contract-geometry lean**, not a statement that Hanwha is the stronger team.

### #2 — Over 9.5
The season H2H has cleared 9.5 in **9/11**, and the two clubs' season scoring baselines total more than 11 runs per game before pitcher adjustment. Hanwha's bullpen is the most important late Over mechanism, while Hilliard's return restores KT's main power threat.

The primary kill path is strong: Daniel has a 2.81 ERA through three starts, White is a 3.31-ERA starter, and KT's latest ten games are 8/10 Under 9.5. That prevents an aggressive Over grade.

### #3 — Under 9.5
Under has a credible first-six-inning structure: Daniel has allowed only five earned runs in 16 innings, White is much better than Hanwha's team pitching baseline, and KT's immediate games have been low scoring. A 5-2 or 6-2 KT win cashes Under while still allowing separation.

It ranks below Over because the direct season matchup is extraordinarily Over-heavy and Hanwha's relief chain has repeatedly created late scoring clusters.

### #4 — KT -1.5
KT has the strongest **directional separation mechanism**: better team, home field, 8-3 H2H, rested bullpen, Hilliard back, and Hanwha's six-game losing streak plus late-relief problems. A 6-3/7-3/8-3 state is highly coherent.

But -1.5 is still the hardest supplied marginal condition. A 5-4 or 6-5 KT win makes the winner call correct and -1.5 wrong. Current same-event prices also place this branch well below Hanwha +1.5 in raw likelihood.

## Ranking logic

### #1 — Lotte +1.5
The immediate team direction strongly favours Samsung, but +1.5 is substantially easier than asking Samsung to win by 2+. Voth's small/volatile KBO sample and 6 ER in three innings against Lotte create a credible one-run or Lotte-upset branch. The concern is Lotte's current four-game skid and only 1/5 +1.5 covers in its L5.

### #2 — Under 10.5
The 10.5 threshold is high enough to absorb common Samsung-winning scores such as 5-3 and 6-4. Voth's last two starts have materially improved and Samsung's relief corps is the strongest late-game suppression mechanism. The counterweight is severe: Daegu averages 10.64 runs/game, Lotte's recent totals are heavily Over, and Rodríguez just allowed 8 ER.

### #3 — Samsung -1.5
This is the strongest team-quality/separation mechanism:
- Samsung is first;
- Lotte is seventh and on four straight losses;
- Samsung's offense and bullpen are clearly stronger;
- Rodríguez carries a real early blowup tail.

It ranks third because -1.5 loses every one-run Samsung game, while Voth's uncertainty makes a 5-4/6-5 state ordinary enough to retain.

### #4 — Over 10.5
The Over has real support from Daegu, Voth's full KBO ERA, Rodríguez's latest outing, Lotte's recent totals and an 11.3-run H2H average. It ranks fourth because 10.5 is demanding, Voth's latest two starts have improved sharply, and Samsung's bullpen can close the scoring window after the middle innings.

Lotte upset path:

- Voth's 6.26 KBO baseline / prior Lotte failure resurfaces,
- Lotte scores 4–6 before Samsung can route the game to its best relievers.

- Dalian: **5.72 for / 4.68 against / 10.40 total**

Chosen line:
**Total Corners Over 8.5**

Mechanism:
Both teams can attack through width and repeated box entries. If the game remains level, both should preserve attacking corner exposure; if one falls behind, chasing behavior supports additional corner volume.

Counter:
A very early multi-goal lead can reduce sustained attacking pressure and kill later corner accumulation.

Provider limitation:
Exact corner grading/provider is not supplied, so this derivative remains capped below the strongest goal markets despite good data coverage.

Primary high-value middle-order all-round batting resource and a major recovery/acceleration mechanism.

## Exact 5-over target process

Market:
**Ireland score at exactly 5 completed legal overs — 20.5**

Required rate to clear:
- 21 runs / 30 legal balls
- equivalent to **4.20 runs per over**

This is a different target from the 50-over innings total.

### Exact/recovered phase examples

Clean exact 5-over data were not available for a sufficiently large recent ODI window. Two recoverable examples:
- Ireland **28/0 after 5** in the 2025 World Cup Qualifier vs Scotland.
- Ireland **41/0 after 5** in the 2024 rain-shortened 22-over ODI chase vs England, but this is NOT a valid like-for-like 50-over batting-first comparator because the required rate and match length were very different.

The 2024 full 50-over ODI against England provides a more useful qualitative new-ball warning:
- Ireland lost two wickets inside the first six overs and were **21/2** by that point before recovering to 210.

### Today's phase-specific negatives
1. England chose to bowl after seeing conditions.
2. Multiple independent Grace Road previews identify early seam/bounce as the most likely early characteristic.
3. Ireland use Alice Tector in the opening role, increasing role uncertainty.
4. England can deploy genuine pace through Filer/Wong/Potts/Corteen-Coleman depending final XI.

### Today's phase-specific positives
1. 20.5 is a low threshold: only 4.2 rpo.
2. Gaby Lewis is experienced at absorbing new-ball pressure.
3. England's attack is missing several first-choice bowlers, especially Bell and Ecclestone.
4. If England miss length or concede wides, the line can be crossed with only 2–3 boundaries.

Phase corridor:
**Ireland approximately 17–24 runs after 5**, with wicket state more important than raw run rate.

Central branch:
**18/1, 19/1, 20/1, 21/1, 22/0**.

This makes the 20.5 threshold genuinely close, but the toss/early-seam state gives the **Under a slight edge**.

2. Final XIs were not officially published in the accessible feeds at freeze.
3. Exact strip report was not recovered.
4. The ETPL is brand-new; team/venue sample sizes are extremely small.
5. Exact Glasgow six-over score vs Edinburgh was not recovered, but the verified score chronology proves it was below 42 and therefore below 46.5.
6. Operator chase-cap, DLS, shortened-match and abandonment settlement terms were not supplied.
7. No fitted/validated cricket numerical model exists.

## Bottom line

1. **Glasgow Cosmic 20-over innings Under 161.5**
2. **Glasgow Cosmic 6-over score Under 46.5**
3. **Glasgow Cosmic 6-over score Over 46.5**
4. **Glasgow Cosmic 20-over innings Over 161.5**
5. **Potential winner: Rotterdam Dockers**

**Logging confirmation:** `P-238/V01` appended before delivery.

**Post-game retrospective:** **NOT PERFORMED** — deferred until explicitly requested.

# Settlement audit and retrospective — P-217 through P-238

Audit date: **2026-09-02**  
Audit scope: **every event in this file, in canonical order**  
Method: official competition/organizer results were preferred; exact phase and derivative markets were checked against scorecards, ball-by-ball or match-stat providers. An official final score settles ordinary side/total markets. Operator-dependent markets are not forced into W/L when the supplied log contains no applicable rule.

## Controlling audit snapshot

| Metric | Audited result | Interpretation |
|---|---:|---|
| Events checked | 22 | P-217 through P-238 |
| Still live | 0 | Every event is final |
| Fully settled logs | 21 | P-218 through P-238 |
| Partially settled logs | 1 | P-217 only |
| Ranked selections | 91 | Four per event except five each in P-233–P-235 |
| Formally settled ranked selections | 89 | 48 wins, 41 losses |
| Operator-dependent selections | 2 | P-217 full-innings U/O 174.5 after reduction to 16 overs |
| Rank #1 record | 13-8, 1 operator-dependent | 61.9% on the 21 formally settled rank-#1 picks |
| At least one top-two pick won | 17/21 fully settled logs | 81.0%; failed in P-220, P-226, P-229 and P-237 |
| Potential-winner record | 15-7 | Descriptive only; no prices or EV claim |

These figures are a descriptive audit, not a validated model backtest. The selections are dependent within each event, the sample is small, and the issued framework explicitly did not publish calibrated probabilities.

No operator or rule was supplied, so neither is entered in the formal W/L count.

## General learnings promoted to the top of the prediction process

| Learning | Evidence in this audit | Required future change |
|---|---|---|
| Separate **result truth**, **prediction correctness**, and **operator grade**. | P-217's actual 185 resolves the directional question but not the sportsbook grade after a 20-to-16-over reduction. | Every cricket market must store operator, exact market wording, minimum-overs/action rule and DLS/shortening rule before it can be called formally settled. |
| A plus-run cushion is not automatically the safest pick when the favourite's separation mechanism is unusually strong. | P-220, P-229, P-231 and P-232 all produced favourite wins by 2+ in low or moderate totals. | Add a **separation-risk gate** using starter edge, bullpen edge, lineup direction, recent 2+ margins and direct H2H separation. If three or more agree, do not promote the underdog +1.5 merely because it covers all close games. |
| Low total and close margin are different targets. | P-220 finished 5-0, P-229 6-1, P-231 3-1 and P-232 3-0. | Build score-allocation branches explicitly. An Under can coexist with a large favourite margin when one side is suppressed. |
| Near-threshold cricket Unders need a boundary-risk audit, not just a central estimate. | P-237 U184.5 lost by 0.5; its paired powerplay Under also lost after 59/0. P-236 U20.5 lost at 23/1. | For a line within roughly 3 runs of a plausible corridor edge, report threshold distance, direct-opponent phase evidence, toss/bat-first state, wicket-resource ceiling and extras volatility. Downgrade when one boundary changes the grade. |
| Direct matchup evidence must be used coherently, not merely acknowledged. | P-237 noted SA's prior 61-run powerplay against the same attack, yet ranked U54.5 second. P-229 documented KT -1.5 in 7/11 H2Hs and Hanwha +1.5 at 0/5, yet ranked Hanwha first. | Add a contradiction table: if the selected side opposes both direct-matchup and shortest-window evidence, it must have a stronger causal reason than market price/contract breadth. Otherwise rank it lower or mark AVOID. |
| Team-strength and winner calls cannot be detached from derivative signals without explanation. | P-223 identified Ureña as the best starter but still chose Yankees; P-235 correctly backed Port to score and the game to go Over, yet selected Shandong to win. | Add a winner/derivative coherence check before issue. If a team-goal Over is high-ranked against a vulnerable opponent, explain why the opponent still wins or revise the winner call. |
| Knockout soccer needs a competition-state adjustment separate from league/H2H scoring history. | P-234's four high-scoring H2Hs and both teams' recent 1H goal rates did not overcome a 0-0 first half and a 1-0 cup result. | Downweight league H2H, model regulation-time caution, and prefer team-specific or corner markets only when current match stats/lineups support them independently. |
| Official score sources should lead; derivative providers should be cross-checked. | MLB, NPB, KBO, ATP and official/major cricket scorecards resolved most endpoints. Corners required specialist feeds. | Source order: official competition/game centre → official team/organizer → major licensed scorecard/data partner → two independent secondary providers. Exact corners or phases should not be graded from a generic result page. |

## P-217 settlement — TKR vs Guyana Amazon Warriors

Verified outcome: **GAW 185/5 in 16 overs; TKR 172/7 in 16; GAW won by 9 runs under DLS. GAW were 31/2 after 6 completed overs.**

| Rank | Issued pick | Realized endpoint | Grade |
|---:|---|---|---|
| 1 | GAW full innings Under 174.5 | 185 in a shortened 16-over innings | **OPERATOR-DEPENDENT**; directional thesis wrong |
| 2 | GAW after 6 Over 46.5 | 31/2 | **LOSS** |
| 3 | GAW after 6 Under 46.5 | 31/2 | **WIN** |
| 4 | GAW full innings Over 174.5 | 185 in a shortened 16-over innings | **OPERATOR-DEPENDENT**; directional thesis right |
| — | Potential winner: GAW | GAW won | **WIN** |

What went right: the winner call survived the rain-adjusted format, and the low early-phase branch was correct. GAW lost two early wickets and were only 31 after six, validating the warning about early restraint/wicket pressure.

What went wrong: the full-innings Under thesis treated a nominal 20-over total as if shortening would merely reduce exposure. In practice, a 16-over game changed batting intent, and Hetmyer produced 111 not out from 43 balls. The same innings therefore had a slow six-over phase and an extreme late acceleration. This is a direct example of the prior cricket lesson that phase and innings totals are separate targets: early wickets did not cap the finisher ceiling.

Process correction: on any shortened T20, freeze the revised overs before interpreting a pre-match full-innings line. Model the new balls-per-wicket/resource state and acceleration ceiling; do not infer the full total from the powerplay. Store the sportsbook's minimum-overs rule before grading.

Sources: [ESPNcricinfo scorecard](https://www.cricinfo.com/series/caribbean-premier-league-2026-1534175/trinbago-knight-riders-vs-guyana-amazon-warriors-22nd-match-1534200/full-scorecard); [CricketWorld scorecard](https://www.cricketworld.com/cricket/trinbago-knight-riders-vs-guyana-amazon-warriors/match/scorecard/96829); [CricketWorld ball-by-ball](https://www.cricketworld.com/cricket/trinbago-knight-riders-vs-guyana-amazon-warriors/match/commentary/96829).

## P-218 settlement — Ann Li vs Antonia Ruzic

Verified outcome: **Li won 7-5, 6-1; 19 total games; Li margin +7.**

| Rank | Issued pick | Grade |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | Li -4.5 games | **WIN** |
| 2 | Under 20.5 games | **WIN** |
| 3 | Over 20.5 games | **LOSS** |
| 4 | Ruzic +4.5 games | **LOSS** |
| — | Potential winner: Li | **WIN** |

Why it worked: the first set was competitive, but Li's stronger sustained level appeared in the second set. A 6-1 second set simultaneously created the handicap cover and kept the match below 20.5. The joint-score reasoning was coherent: the same straight-sets separation branch carried both top picks.

Learning: for tennis spread/total pairs, identify whether the primary route is one lopsided set or two moderate breaks. Here, the lopsided-set route was decisive and should continue to be mapped explicitly.

Source: [Tennis.com completed match page](https://www.tennis.com/tournaments/us-open/matches/a-li-vs-a-ruzic-2026-08-31).

## P-219 settlement — Mets @ Rays

Verified outcome: **Mets 3, Rays 2; 5 total runs.**

| Rank | Issued pick | Grade |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | Mets +1.5 | **WIN** |
| 2 | Over 7.5 | **LOSS** |
| 3 | Rays -1.5 | **LOSS** |
| 4 | Under 7.5 | **WIN** |
| — | Potential winner: Rays | **LOSS** |

What went right: the top-ranked cushion was robust and the Under branch existed in the original tree. Robert Stock delivered 5.1 scoreless innings; Ian Seymour struck out ten but allowed the three Mets runs. The game stayed close, exactly the environment in which Mets +1.5 was strongest.

What went wrong: the potential-winner call over-weighted Tampa's broader team/starter baseline and under-weighted the chance that Stock's weak season line would not describe this single start. The Over rank also leaned too much on Stock's upper tail; his actual command suppressed that path.

Learning: when the #1 pick is an underdog cushion and the opposing favourite's -1.5 is ranked below it, the winner call should carry only modest confidence. A starter with a poor baseline still needs an explicit quality-start probability branch.

Source: [ESPN final and box score](https://www.espn.com/mlb/game/_/gameId/401816748/mets-rays).

## P-220 settlement and deep rank-#1 retrospective — Padres @ Reds

Verified outcome: **Padres 5, Reds 0; 5 total runs. Michael King threw six scoreless innings; Brady Singer allowed four runs on ten hits in 4.1 innings.**

| Rank | Issued pick | Grade |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | Reds +1.5 | **LOSS** |
| 2 | Over 9.0 | **LOSS** |
| 3 | Padres -1.5 | **WIN** |
| 4 | Under 9.0 | **WIN** |
| — | Potential winner: Padres | **WIN** |

What went right: the analysis correctly identified King as the strongest run-suppression component, Singer as the weaker starter, San Diego as the better winner side, and Cincinnati's bullpen as the more vulnerable late unit. It also explicitly named a 5-2/6-2 Padres separation branch. The winner call, Padres -1.5 and Under all came from those sound mechanisms.

Why the “most likely” pick failed: the ranking let contract breadth and recent close-game counts outrank the strongest causal matchup. Cincinnati +1.5 was supported by the fact that it covers every Reds win and one-run loss, but the actual game was never a close-allocation problem. King extended his scoreless streak to 15.2 innings and recorded a fifth straight quality start. Singer then allowed six consecutive hits in a four-run fourth. Once Cincinnati produced zero, even a moderate Padres output created automatic separation.

The pre-game evidence already contained the correction: King had a 3.23 ERA and strong recent form; Singer had a 4.72 ERA, 1.42 WHIP and five homers allowed in five August starts; Padres relief had been excellent; Reds relief was weak. The ranking understated the covariance of these edges. It also over-weighted Cincinnati's previous-day four-homer result, a noisy recency signal against a different starter.

Required improvement:

1. When favourite starter edge + bullpen edge + winner edge all align, compute a favourite shutout/one-run-conceded branch before promoting the dog cushion.
2. Do not translate “many recent close games” across a materially different starting-pitcher matchup without opponent adjustment.
3. For integer totals, rank the Under above the Over when the best starter and best bullpen sit on the same side and the opponent has a realistic 0–2 run distribution; hot weather alone is not enough to reverse it.
4. In this card, the evidence-consistent order should have been closer to **Under 9 / Padres -1.5 / Reds +1.5 / Over 9**, with the exact first two order depending on the desired marginal target.

Sources: [Reuters game report](https://www.reuters.com/sports/baseball/padres-pile-fourth-shut-down-reds-opener--flm-2026-09-01/); [ESPN recap and box score](https://www.espn.com/mlb/recap/_/gameId/401816749); [CBS box score](https://www.cbssports.com/mlb/gametracker/recap/MLB_20260831_SD%40CIN/).

## P-221 settlement — Marlins @ Nationals

Verified outcome: **Nationals 6, Marlins 3; 9 total runs.**

| Rank | Issued pick | Grade |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | Nationals +1.5 | **WIN** |
| 2 | Over 8.5 | **WIN** |
| 3 | Under 8.5 | **LOSS** |
| 4 | Marlins -1.5 | **LOSS** |
| — | Potential winner: Marlins | **LOSS** |

What went right: the Washington cushion and Over were aligned with the pre-game concern about unusual pitching architecture and bullpen exposure. Nine runs cleared the line by the minimum half-run, and Washington won outright.

What went wrong: the Marlins winner call was inconsistent with the higher-ranked Nationals +1.5 and with the identified Washington offensive upside. The log correctly warned that the Nationals' starter/bullpen uncertainty widened both tails, but it treated that mainly as Miami upside rather than two-sided variance.

Learning: when a dog cushion ranks first and the total is expected high, the dog outright branch deserves more weight because variance makes separation against it less reliable. Winner confidence should be reduced or the winner should follow the top side signal.

Sources: [ESPN final](https://www.espn.com/mlb/game/_/gameId/401816747/marlins-nationals); [MLB game story](https://www.mlb.com/stories/game/822689).

Learning: this is a positive example of contract geometry used correctly. A favourite winner and underdog +1.5 can coexist when the forecast explicitly expects a competitive, volatile game and treats -1.5 as the hardest condition.

## P-223 settlement — Yankees @ Angels

Verified outcome: **Angels 10, Yankees 1; 11 total runs. Walbert Ureña allowed one run over seven innings; Elmer Rodríguez allowed four runs in 3.2 innings.**

| Rank | Issued pick | Grade |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | Angels +1.5 | **WIN** |
| 2 | Yankees moneyline | **LOSS** |
| 3 | Under 8.0 | **LOSS** |
| 4 | Over 8.0 | **WIN** |
| — | Potential winner: Yankees | **LOSS** |

What went right: Angels +1.5 respected the game's best established starter. The Over branch also correctly captured the rookie-starter/bullpen failure tail.

What went wrong: the winner call privileged overall team strength over the most important game-specific variable even though the original text said Ureña was the strongest established starter. Rodríguez's short outing exposed the Yankees' run-prevention tail, while Ureña removed the comeback route.

Learning: if the underdog owns a large confirmed starter edge and is already rank #1 with +1.5, a favourite moneyline/winner call requires a clearly superior bullpen and lineup edge large enough to overcome that starter gap. Otherwise the card is internally incoherent.

Source: [ESPN recap](https://www.espn.com/mlb/recap/_/gameId/401816756).

## P-226 settlement and deep rank-#1 retrospective — Hanshin @ Yakult

Verified outcome: **Hanshin 6, Yakult 2; 8 total runs; Hanshin won by four.**

| Rank | Issued pick | Grade |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | Under 7.5 | **LOSS** |
| 2 | Yakult +2.5 | **LOSS** |
| 3 | Hanshin -2.5 | **WIN** |
| 4 | Over 7.5 | **WIN** |
| — | Potential winner: Hanshin | **WIN** |

What went right: the team-quality and starter-direction analysis correctly preferred Hanshin. The original card explicitly identified Hanshin's 67-49-1 record, Takahashi's large starter advantage, league-best team ERA and Hanshin's 12-6 season H2H edge. Those signals supported both the winner and the 2+ separation path.

Why rank #1 failed: the Under was based on strong historical counts—Hanshin games 9/10 Under and recent H2H 8/9 Under—but the 7.5 threshold left no margin for an ordinary 6-2 favourite win. Jingu's hitter-friendly environment and Yoshimura's 4.39 ERA/5.10 recent-five ERA were known upper-tail factors. The analysis treated the favourite's run-production ceiling as a remote tail even though the same team-quality evidence used for the winner supported a 5–7 run Hanshin output.

Why rank #2 also failed: Yakult +2.5 was paired too tightly with the Under. A low or moderate total does not imply a close score. Hanshin's superior starter could suppress Yakult while Yoshimura allowed enough for separation—the exact 6-2 state.

Required improvement:

1. At totals of 7.5 or lower, calculate one-sided 5-1/6-1/6-2 states separately; do not equate a low league environment with close margins.
2. When winner confidence is strong and the underdog +2.5 has only a thin offensive ceiling against an elite starter, the favourite run line should move above the dog cushion.
3. Downweight raw H2H Under frequency when today's weaker starter and park environment create a plausible favourite-only scoring expansion.
4. A better pre-game order from the stated evidence would have been **Hanshin -2.5 / Under 7.5 / Over 7.5 / Yakult +2.5**, with all still capped because exact probabilities were unavailable.

Source: [official NPB September 1 scores](https://npb.jp/bis/eng/2026/games/).
