## P-229 settlement and deep rank-#1 retrospective — Hanwha @ KT

Verified outcome: **KT 6, Hanwha 1; 7 total runs; KT won by five.**

| Rank | Issued pick | Grade |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | Hanwha +1.5 | **LOSS** |
| 2 | Over 9.5 | **LOSS** |
| 3 | Under 9.5 | **WIN** |
| 4 | KT -1.5 | **WIN** |
| — | Potential winner: KT | **WIN** |

What went right: the research found almost every decisive mechanism. KT was 66-43-3 against Hanwha's 49-62-3, led the H2H 8-3, had the stronger/rested bullpen, returned Sam Hilliard, and faced a Hanwha team on a six-game losing streak. The card explicitly described KT's 5-2/6-2 low-total separation branch—the realized 6-1 score was nearly identical.

Why the “most likely” pick failed: Hanwha +1.5 was elevated mainly because it was the shortest-priced and broadest mathematical contract. That ignored the evidence's direction and severity. The log itself recorded Hanwha +1.5 at **0/5 and 5/20**, KT -1.5 at **7/11 H2Hs**, and a major late-inning edge for KT. A contract cannot be called the most robust merely because it covers one-run outcomes when the matchup repeatedly produces multi-run KT wins.

Why rank #2 failed: the Over leaned heavily on 9/11 H2H Overs and team season scoring, but the current starter state pointed Under: Daniel had a 2.81 ERA, White was much better than Hanwha's team ERA, and KT's latest ten were 8/10 Under. Once KT controlled Hanwha, the weak Hanwha offense could not contribute enough to reach 10.

Required improvement:

1. Treat market price as a baseline, not a veto over strong matchup evidence. Document when the model view disagrees with price instead of mechanically ranking the shortest price first.
2. Add a contradiction penalty: a pick opposed by L5, L20, direct H2H, winner direction and bullpen direction cannot rank first without a specific new causal change.
3. Score one-sided totals. A weak bullpen can help KT separation while the game remains Under if Hanwha scores only 0–2.
4. The evidence-consistent order should have been **KT -1.5 / Under 9.5 / Hanwha +1.5 / Over 9.5**. The original analysis contained that answer but the ranking did not follow it.

Sources: [official KBO results](https://eng.koreabaseball.com/Schedule/DailySchedule.aspx); [MyKBO game box](https://mykbostats.com/games/13892-Hanwha-vs-KT-20260901).

## P-230 settlement — KIA @ NC

Verified outcome: **NC 7, KIA 2; 9 total runs; NC scored four in the seventh.**

| Rank | Issued pick | Grade |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | NC +1.5 | **WIN** |
| 2 | KIA moneyline | **LOSS** |
| 3 | Over 9.5 | **LOSS** |
| 4 | Under 9.5 | **WIN** |
| — | Potential winner: KIA | **LOSS** |

What went right: NC +1.5 correctly captured the matchup-specific left-handed starter/H2H risk, and the Under survived at nine runs.

What went wrong: the KIA winner call overrode the same NC-specific evidence that made NC the top handicap. The four-run seventh again shows how the relief transition can turn a competitive game into separation without pushing a 9.5 total Over.

Learning: if a team's cushion is #1 because the opponent has a specific matchup problem, the opposite winner call needs a stronger, explicit reason. Otherwise align the winner with the highest-ranked side or lower its confidence.

Sources: [official KBO results](https://eng.koreabaseball.com/Schedule/DailySchedule.aspx); [MyKBO game box](https://mykbostats.com/games/13893-Kia-vs-NC-20260901).

## P-231 settlement and deep rank-#1 retrospective — LG @ Doosan

Verified outcome: **LG 3, Doosan 1; 4 total runs; LG won by exactly two.**

| Rank | Issued pick | Grade |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | Doosan +1.5 | **LOSS** |
| 2 | Under 8.5 | **WIN** |
| 3 | LG +0.5 | **WIN** |
| 4 | Over 8.5 | **LOSS** |
| — | Potential winner: LG | **WIN** |

What went right: the starter/H2H/park analysis was strong. Im Chan-kyu's current form and 2.04 ERA versus Doosan, Logue's credible but weaker state, Jamsil's run suppression and a user total above the market centre all pointed to LG plus Under. The winner, Under and LG +0.5 all won.

Why rank #1 failed: Doosan +1.5 was promoted on recent cover counts (18/20) and contract breadth even though the best game-specific evidence pointed to a low-scoring LG win. At low totals, a two-run margin is not rare: 3-1, 4-2 and 5-3 are natural results. The exact 3-1 final was already represented by the original “LG controlled separation” logic, yet that branch was not weighted enough.

This was a threshold failure by the minimum margin: Doosan +1.5 needed a one-run loss and lost by two. That does not make the underlying close-game logic absurd, but it does show the ranking was too confident relative to the line's sensitivity.

Required improvement:

1. For +1.5 at a low total, measure the probability of the favourite winning by exactly two; do not bundle all low-score outcomes into “close.”
2. Use opponent-adjusted cover windows. Doosan's 18/20 record was not conditioned on facing the hottest starter in the matchup.
3. When winner + starter + Under all align, elevate the winner-aligned side above the opposing cushion unless the dog has a clear scoring floor.
4. A better order was **Under 8.5 / LG +0.5 / Doosan +1.5 / Over 8.5**.

Sources: [official KBO results](https://eng.koreabaseball.com/Schedule/DailySchedule.aspx); [MyKBO game box](https://mykbostats.com/games/13894-LG-vs-Doosan-20260901).

## P-232 settlement and deep rank-#1 retrospective — Lotte @ Samsung

Verified outcome: **Samsung 3, Lotte 0; 3 total runs; Samsung won by three.**

| Rank | Issued pick | Grade |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | Lotte +1.5 | **LOSS** |
| 2 | Under 10.5 | **WIN** |
| 3 | Samsung -1.5 | **WIN** |
| 4 | Over 10.5 | **LOSS** |
| — | Potential winner: Samsung | **WIN** |

What went right: Samsung's winner case, bullpen edge and the high 10.5 Under threshold were correctly identified. Voth and the Samsung relief chain shut Lotte out, producing the low-total favourite-separation branch.

Why rank #1 failed: Lotte +1.5 again rose because it was the easier/shorter-priced handicap, while the causal evidence favoured Samsung by multiple runs. Samsung entered first, on five straight wins, with the better offense, team ERA and a 2.92 relief ERA versus Lotte's 3.88. Lotte had lost four straight and covered +1.5 only 1/5. Voth's small sample created uncertainty, but the analysis overweighted his prior six-run failure against Lotte and underweighted his 20 strikeouts against one walk over the latest two starts.

The rain delay was correctly treated as uncertainty, but it should not have been used implicitly to favor the underdog. Once the game started, Samsung's superior suppression structure was the clearest path.

Required improvement:

1. Use recent pitcher trend as a modifier with skill indicators, not ERA alone. Voth's strikeout/command improvement was a real regime signal.
2. When the favourite has team, bullpen, home, form and winner alignment, an underdog cushion needs a verified offensive floor before it can rank first.
3. Explicitly pair Under with both close and separated allocations. Here 3-0/4-1 was at least as coherent as 4-3/5-4.
4. A better order was **Under 10.5 / Samsung -1.5 / Lotte +1.5 / Over 10.5**.

Sources: [official KBO results](https://eng.koreabaseball.com/Schedule/DailySchedule.aspx); [MyKBO game box](https://mykbostats.com/games/13895-Lotte-vs-Samsung-20260901).

## P-233 settlement — Beijing Guoan vs Lanzhou Longyuan

Verified outcome: **Beijing 3, Lanzhou 1; halftime 1-0; 13 corners (Beijing 9, Lanzhou 4).**

| Rank | Issued pick | Grade |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | Beijing team goals Over 1.5 | **WIN** |
| 2 | 1st-half Over 0.5 goals | **WIN** |
| 3 | Total goals Over 2.5 | **WIN** |
| 4 | BTTS — No | **LOSS** |
| 5 | Corners Over 8.5 | **WIN** |
| — | Potential winner: Beijing | **WIN** |

What went right: the core mismatch and Beijing scoring ceiling were correctly prioritized. Beijing scored three, an early goal landed the first-half derivative, and sustained attack volume produced 13 corners.

What went wrong: BTTS No treated Beijing dominance as if it guaranteed a clean sheet. Lanzhou's single goal did not contradict the mismatch; it only showed that winner/team-total strength and clean-sheet probability are different.

Learning: avoid using a large favourite's attacking superiority as direct evidence for BTTS No. Model the underdog's one-goal route separately through transition, set piece and late game-state relaxation.

Sources: [Flashscore result](https://www.flashscoreusa.com/game/soccer/beijing-guoan-WSLjVBLN/lanzhou-longyuan-athletic-vq7b83U1/); [SoccerPunter match data](https://www.soccerpunter.com/match/19726928/Beijing-Guoan-vs-Lanzhou-Longyuan).

## P-234 settlement and deep rank-#1 retrospective — Dalian Yingbo vs Shanghai Shenhua

Verified outcome: **Dalian 1, Shanghai Shenhua 0; halftime 0-0; goal in the 54th minute; 10 corners (5-5).**

| Rank | Issued pick | Grade |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | 1st-half Over 0.5 goals | **LOSS** |
| 2 | Dalian team goals Over 0.5 | **WIN** |
| 3 | Corners Over 8.5 | **WIN** |
| 4 | BTTS — Yes | **LOSS** |
| 5 | Total goals Over 2.5 | **LOSS** |
| — | Potential winner: Shanghai Shenhua | **LOSS** |

What went right: Dalian's team-goal line was a strong, target-specific read. The pre-game log noted Dalian had scored in 5/5 and Shanghai had conceded in 4/5. Dalian scored once, exactly matching that modest team-specific thesis. The corner model was also independent and correct: both sides took five.

Why the “most likely” pick failed: the 1H Over relied on 4/5 recent first-half hit rates for each team, 4/4 H2Hs, and short market pricing. Those facts were real but overfit a league/H2H scoring regime to a knockout quarter-final. Dalian's own full-match state was low-event—only 1/5 recent matches went Over 2.5—and the log acknowledged cup caution as the kill path. That kill path was more central than the rank implied.

Why the wider goal view failed: high-scoring H2Hs (5-3, 4-1, 2-2, 3-0) were not sufficiently adjusted for current competition state. Shanghai had more possession/territorial expectation but did not score; the only goal was a 54th-minute Dalian penalty. BTTS and Over 2.5 therefore shared the same unsupported assumption that Shanghai's volatile attack would convert.

Why the winner call failed: the analysis made Shanghai a modest favourite based on H2H and team quality, but Dalian's current home scoring floor and compact recent form deserved more weight in regulation-time cup play. “To advance” and 90-minute winner also needed sharper separation.

Required improvement:

1. Apply a knockout-regulation modifier before using league/H2H first-half frequencies.
2. When one team's recent full-match state is strongly low-event, do not allow the opponent's volatile Over history to dominate all goal derivatives.
3. Keep team-goal and corner targets independent; this card demonstrates that Dalian O0.5 and corners O8.5 can win while 1H Over, BTTS and full Over all lose.
4. A more evidence-balanced order was **Dalian team O0.5 / corners O8.5 / 1H O0.5 / BTTS Yes / O2.5**, with Shanghai regulation winner only a low-confidence lean.

Sources: [Sofascore event page](https://www.sofascore.com/football/match/dalian-yingbo-fc-shanghai-shenhua/yrbsRZOd); [BeSoccer match statistics](https://www.besoccer.com/match/dalian-zhixing/shanghai-shenhua/2026345784).

## P-235 settlement — Shandong Taishan vs Shanghai Port

Verified outcome: **Shandong 0, Shanghai Port 3; halftime 0-2; goals in the 18th, 22nd and 79th minutes; 14 corners.**

| Rank | Issued pick | Grade |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | 1st-half Over 0.5 goals | **WIN** |
| 2 | Shanghai Port team goals Over 0.5 | **WIN** |
| 3 | BTTS — Yes | **LOSS** |
| 4 | Total goals Over 2.5 | **WIN** |
| 5 | Corners Over 8.5 | **WIN** |
| — | Potential winner: Shandong | **LOSS** |

What went right: Port's team-goal line, first-half goal signal, full Over and corner volume all captured the visitors' attacking control. Two early Port goals made the phase read decisive; a third completed the Over.

What went wrong: BTTS assumed Shandong would contribute, but Port's clean sheet created a one-sided Over. The Shandong winner call was especially incoherent with a high-ranked Port team-goal and high-event game view. Port scoring was not merely compatible with a Shandong win; it was evidence against it once combined with Shandong's vulnerability.

Learning: add a winner/derivative coherence audit. A team-goal Over plus opponent defensive concerns should move the scoring team upward in winner probability unless the home side has a clearly larger scoring projection.

Sources: [Sofascore event page](https://www.sofascore.com/football/match/shanghai-port-shandong-taishan/wrbsMFq); [Scoremer event timeline](https://www.scoremer.com/match_live/1636436).

## P-236 settlement and deep rank-#1 retrospective — England Women vs Ireland Women, 1st ODI

Verified outcome: **Ireland 23/1 after 5 overs and 281/5 after 50; England 283/4 in 38.1 and won by six wickets.**

| Rank | Issued pick | Grade |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | Ireland after 5 Under 20.5 | **LOSS** |
| 2 | Ireland innings Over 218.5 | **WIN** |
| 3 | Ireland innings Under 218.5 | **LOSS** |
| 4 | Ireland after 5 Over 20.5 | **WIN** |
| — | Potential winner: England | **WIN** |

What went right: the innings-total analysis correctly placed 218.5 below Ireland's recent batting-first centre. Ireland's Lewis/Hunter/Prendergast core and England's understrength attack supported a recovery/acceleration route, and Ireland went well beyond the line at 281/5. England's depth and chase advantage supported the winner call.

Why the “most likely” pick failed: the 5-over Under had weak exact-sample support and a very tight threshold—21 runs from 30 balls. Ireland were 13/0 after four; Issy Wong then produced a costly fifth over containing four wides plus a boundary, and the score reached 23/1. The prediction correctly named extras and one expensive over as the kill path, but still ranked the Under first with only medium-low evidence.

This is a calibration/ranking error more than a research omission. The log admitted that an L5/L10 exact phase sample could not be reconstructed and set a central corridor of 17–24, which straddled both sides of 20.5. A pick whose own central corridor crosses the line and whose evidence is medium-low should not be called the strongest selection ahead of the better-supported innings Over.

Required improvement:

1. A phase pick cannot rank #1 when the central interval spans both sides of the line unless one side has materially greater modeled mass.
2. Add an extras-risk term for new/understrength bowling attacks; wides count fully toward phase totals and can invalidate otherwise sound-ball suppression.
3. Prefer the higher-evidence innings target when the phase sample is sparse. Here **Ireland innings Over 218.5** should have ranked first.
4. Preserve the valuable phase/innings distinction: the quick fifth over did not cause England to lose; Ireland's 281 and England's successful chase were separate resource questions.

Sources: [CricketWorld scorecard](https://www.cricketworld.com/cricket/england-women-vs-ireland-women/match/scorecard/95250); [CricketWorld ball-by-ball](https://www.cricketworld.com/cricket/england-women-vs-ireland-women/match/commentary/95250); [Guardian match report](https://www.theguardian.com/sport/2026/sep/01/maia-bouchier-powers-england-to-record-odi-run-chase-against-ireland).

## P-237 settlement and deep rank-#1 retrospective — South Africa vs Zimbabwe

Verified outcome: **South Africa 59/0 after 6 and 185/7 after 20; Zimbabwe 142; South Africa won by 43 runs.**

| Rank | Issued pick | Grade |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | South Africa innings Under 184.5 | **LOSS** |
| 2 | South Africa after 6 Under 54.5 | **LOSS** |
| 3 | South Africa after 6 Over 54.5 | **WIN** |
| 4 | South Africa innings Over 184.5 | **WIN** |
| — | Potential winner: South Africa | **WIN** |

What went right: the winner call was strong. South Africa's batting ceiling, rest advantage and Zimbabwe's recent run-prevention volatility were correctly identified. The card also documented the decisive direct evidence: the same batting group had made 61 in the powerplay against the same opponent at the same venue three days earlier.

Why the “most likely” pick failed: Under 184.5 lost by only **0.5 run**, but the closeness of the grade does not excuse the ranking. The line was just 2.5 runs below a recent South Africa 187 and the venue had just produced 195 and 190. Zimbabwe had less than 24 hours' recovery, and South Africa batted first—the exposure state most dangerous to an innings Under. After a 59/0 powerplay, the full resource ceiling was high. South Africa reached 154/1 after 15.1 overs and still finished 185 despite a late collapse to 185/7.

Why rank #2 failed: recent aggregate powerplays (5/6 Under) were allowed to outweigh the most comparable observation: 61 against this exact attack at this exact venue. That is a source-weighting error. Direct opponent/venue/role continuity should outrank a six-match cross-opponent mean when the current bowling fatigue state is also worse.

The result links to the prior cricket lesson: wicket resources matter as much as run rate. At 59/0, South Africa had all ten wickets intact, which made sustained acceleration possible. The late wickets nearly rescued the Under but did not erase the earlier resource advantage.

Required improvement:

1. After toss confirmation that South Africa bat first, re-rank the full-innings Over upward; remove the chase-cap concern that had supported Under/action uncertainty.
2. Give exact opponent + venue phase evidence the highest weight when lineups/roles remain materially continuous.
3. For a half-run line at the edge of the upper corridor, run a boundary audit. A central 160–180 corridor is not enough when credible states 185–195 are recent and directly relevant.
4. Add wicket-resource state to linked phase trees: 55/0 is substantially more dangerous for an innings Under than 55/3.
5. The evidence-consistent order after toss should have been **SA after 6 Over 54.5 / SA innings Over 184.5 / U184.5 / U54.5**, all capped for small samples.

Sources: [ESPNcricinfo scorecard](https://www.cricinfo.com/series/namibia-t20i-tri-series-aug-2026-2026-1549518/south-africa-vs-zimbabwe-4th-match-1549527/full-scorecard); [Hindustan Times scorecard and match flow](https://www.hindustantimes.com/cricket/live-full-scorecard-zim-vs-sa-t20i-tri-series-in-namibia-2026-match-4-zimbabwe-vs-south-africa-t20-zmsa09012026273843); [CricketWorld scorecard](https://www.cricketworld.com/cricket/south-africa-vs-zimbabwe/match/scorecard/98364).

## P-238 settlement — Glasgow Cosmic vs Rotterdam Dockers

Verified outcome: **Glasgow 39/2 after 6 and 140/9 after 20; Rotterdam 141/5 in 19.5 and won by five wickets.**

| Rank | Issued pick | Grade |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | Glasgow innings Under 161.5 | **WIN** |
| 2 | Glasgow after 6 Under 46.5 | **WIN** |
| 3 | Glasgow after 6 Over 46.5 | **LOSS** |
| 4 | Glasgow innings Over 161.5 | **LOSS** |
| — | Potential winner: Rotterdam | **WIN** |

Why it worked: the phase and full-innings Unders were aligned with Glasgow's limited sample, Rotterdam's bowling resources and the uncertainty around a new competition. Glasgow lost two powerplay wickets, never created a sustained acceleration state, and finished 21.5 runs below the innings line. Rotterdam then completed the expected chase.

Learning: this is the desired cricket structure—phase, wickets and innings resource path all agreed. The evidence was still sample-capped, but the ranking did not overstate a fragile boundary line.

Sources: [ESPN scorecard](https://www.espn.com/cricket/series/24698/scorecard/1547880/glasgow-cosmic-vs-rotterdam-dockers-9th-match-european-t20-premier-league-2026); [official ETPL match page](https://www.etplofficial.com/matches/6a688c61b30844b0969df8ab).

## Source-quality audit and improved source plan

| Sport / market | Best sources used here | Reliability assessment | Better future practice |
|---|---|---|---|
| MLB finals, innings, player lines | MLB, ESPN/AP, Reuters, CBS | **High** when MLB/box score leads; reports explain mechanism | Store MLB game ID and official box/play-by-play first; use Reuters/AP only for narrative context. |
| NPB finals | Official NPB daily/game pages | **High** | Use exact official game page rather than only the day index when inning/bullpen mechanism matters. |
| KBO finals | Official English KBO schedule; MyKBO boxes | **High for final score; medium-high for detailed secondary box** | Make official KBO GameCenter the first endpoint and retain MyKBO only for accessible inning/player detail. |
| Tennis | ATP official results; established tournament match pages | **High** | Prefer ATP/WTA/ITF official draw and completed score; use a second source only for retirement/stat detail. |
| Cricket final/phase | ESPNcricinfo, official competition page, CricketWorld ball-by-ball | **High for score/phase when feeds agree** | Save exact innings/over endpoint plus toss and revised overs. For operator settlement, capture book rules separately; a scorecard cannot determine a void rule. |
| Chinese FA Cup goals | Sofascore/Flashscore plus secondary match-stat pages | **Medium-high for final score; medium for derivatives** | Seek CFA/club official match report first. Cross-check corners with two licensed/stat providers because generic result pages often omit or revise derivatives. |
| Corners | BeSoccer, SoccerPunter, Scoremer cross-checks | **Medium** | Record provider and retrieval time. Do not grade if providers disagree; exact sportsbook provider rules may still control. |

## Final audit conclusions

1. **No pending event remains live.**
2. **P-218 through P-238 are fully settled.**
3. **P-217 is only partially settled** because its two full-innings selections require an operator's shortened-match rule; its actual innings and six-over outcome are fully known.
4. The largest process weakness was not missing research but **ranking incoherence**: contract breadth/short price sometimes overrode the strongest starter, bullpen, direct-matchup and winner mechanisms.
5. The most important sport-specific correction is to treat **one-sided low-score separation** as ordinary in baseball and to treat **wicket resources, toss/exposure, direct-opponent phase evidence and threshold distance** as mandatory in cricket.
6. The strongest positive pattern was target separation: several cards correctly distinguished winner from handicap, or phase from innings. That structure should be retained while improving the weighting and coherence gates above.

**Settlement logging confirmation:** audit appended without altering any frozen pre-game card. Formal aggregate: **48 wins, 41 losses, 2 operator-dependent ranked selections; rank #1 = 13-8 with 1 operator-dependent; potential winners = 15-7.**
